Handoff verification s3://data-collection-059416028963-us-east-1-an/research-data/constellation-backfill/deployments/temporary-archive-intake/handoff-provenance/completed-bef622ac2df1a10c1f2c4093adf3c26201ed9ed1f5af3e28b55d4a4dcd91b552.json

Daily describes automated collection methods, including recurring release checks where the publisher releases less often.
Monthly identifies manual downloads or release checks on the first calendar day (UTC). Collect quarterly and annual files only when a new or revised edition is available. See market-monthly-download-checklist.md.
Committed counts are from optima_collected_data.committed_records, with batch files counted from the same published manifests.
File-inventory counts are from optima_regulatory_pilot.constellation_public_artifacts. Provider originals are counted by distinct S3 URI.
Labelled counts are source-wide where indicated and come from the saved verified publication ledger. Archive-specific counts use their publication receipts and state the dataset scope.
Saved metadata publication counts 2026-11-25T05:30:07+00:00 — s3://data-collection-059416028963-us-east-1-an/research-data/monitoring/health/metadata-publication-counts.json
Grouped metadata Athena verification 2026-11-24T23:10:11+00:00 — 900cac1b-b1f2-48fd-bf46-c8b6737c3be1
The Athena views can overlap. A zero applies to the views checked.
Outstanding counts are distinct raw input paths in the metadata queue, including queued, running, retrying, uncommitted and raw-validation failures.
Files the metadata queue has yet to discover are outside that count. USPTO queue counts use the dataset path where available.

First raw publication uses the earliest provider publication date verified in the evidence reviewed for this entry.
Raw file descriptions identify the corresponding evidence or a retained sample when the publication date is unverified.
Raw evidence links are examples of retained provider files or supplied archives of provider originals. The S3 links require project access. The examples need not match the first publication date.
Evidence examples verified 2026-11-26 UTC. 542 object links across 222 entries. 147 entries have three verified examples, 75 have one or two, and 47 have none verified in the files checked.
Publication dates retain the provider’s precision. Download dates, reporting periods and event dates are excluded.

METADATA PROCESSING VERSION KEY | Proposed source routing, 2026-11-26 UTC
The Metadata version line pins the primary profile for each source after the upstream metadata job is committed.
These are planned assignments for one combined metadata and semiotic engine with a shared output contract.
Shared stages include identity resolution, language detection before translation and thesis, and object-first ISIC with actor then source fallback.
They do not imply deployed support, collection access, or a change to the existing Metadata status.
A source can contain multiple artifact types. Apply the indicated secondary profile when relevant, or route by verified native format.
Where the inventory has no verified raw evidence links, the assigned route is provisional until an original is inspected.
For news and press releases, use the complete original publisher item. A headline, abstract, or API excerpt is discovery only.
Use the inventory ID as well as the source key. Sources 040, 063 and 064 share the uspto_odp key.

ME-01@1.0.0 | Full news article — Use original article text with publisher, byline, and dates. API and RSS links are discovery.
ME-02@1.0.0 | Press release or public announcement — Use the complete publisher release, in HTML or PDF, with attachments where substantive.
ME-03@1.0.0 | Scholarly full-text XML — Read the complete article, including sections, tables, and reference boundaries.
ME-04@1.0.0 | Scholarly discovery to full text — Resolve the citation or catalogue entry to the original article, thesis, or preprint. Do not analyze abstracts as full text.
ME-05@1.0.0 | Original SEC submission — Parse the complete submission and relevant exhibits. The issuer or EDGAR index is not the filing.
ME-06@1.0.0 | XBRL statements and facts — Extract concepts, units, fiscal periods, contexts, and filing references. Preserve original attachments.
ME-07@1.0.0 | Issuer or ownership disclosure package — Use the full filed documents or the original regulator bulk release. Keep index rows and filed records distinct.
ME-08@1.0.0 | Official PDF document or report — Read the publisher PDF, retaining page, table, and cell references. Use OCR when necessary.
ME-09@1.0.0 | Official HTML document — Read the complete publisher document. Retain section and table anchors and resolve substantive attachments.
ME-10@1.0.0 | Clinical, regulatory, or legal record — Parse the native record or case document and preserve regulatory identifiers, statuses, roles, and attachments. Follow discovery links where needed.
ME-11@1.0.0 | Procurement and awards — Parse native notice, lot, award, and place-of-performance fields, retaining source extensions and archive members.
ME-12@1.0.0 | Time series and statistical API — Preserve series keys, observation times, units, revisions, and release vintage where supplied.
ME-13@1.0.0 | SDMX datasets — Read series keys, dimensions, attributes, units, and observation vintages from the native SDMX response.
ME-14@1.0.0 | Native API or structured records — Use native JSON, XML, or record envelopes, preserving identifiers and original field paths.
ME-15@1.0.0 | Delimited dataset — Parse native CSV, TSV, or delimited text without losing original fields, units, notes, or reporting dates.
ME-16@1.0.0 | Workbook or native tabular report — Preserve workbook, sheet, row, and column provenance and capture units, headings, and reported methodology.
ME-17@1.0.0 | Bulk release package — Preserve the original archive and manifest. Validate and route member files using their native formats.
ME-18@1.0.0 | Geospatial feature or grid — Retain native geometries, coordinate systems, tiles, timestamps, measurements, and geographic roles.
ME-19@1.0.0 | Catalogue or repository index — Catalogued item metadata is valid for inventory and relationships. Retrieve originals before extracting document-level claims.
ME-20@1.0.0 | Market, trade, shipping or holdings data — Read native API rows, official tables, or release files for prices, positions, volumes, and commercial flows. Preserve instruments, venues, units, periods, and revisions.
ME-21@1.0.0 | Patent publication or prosecution record — Preserve application and publication IDs, actors, assignments, claims, and procedural events. Read linked originals for document-level extraction.
ME-22@1.0.0 | Social post, comment, or discussion — Preserve complete public posts or comments, authors, thread context, and post timestamps.
ME-23@1.0.0 | Advertising and creative evidence — Read native campaign and delivery fields. Link original creative for content analysis and flag missing assets.
ME-24@1.0.0 | Survey and specialty data format — Use native statistical readers for RDS, SAS transport, or survey releases. Keep codebooks, weights, and sample definitions.
ME-25@1.0.0 | Entity registry and organization directory — Read native record IDs, identifiers, names, roles, and reported activities. Use document parsing when the same provider publishes reports.
ME-26@1.0.0 | Web or network capture — Preserve capture timestamps and native WARC, crawl, or MRT record boundaries. Distinguish archive metadata from replayed content.
ME-27@1.0.0 | Mixed data and publication release — Identify the native artifact first, then use the matching data or document profile. Hold unsupported formats for inspection.
